---

## 1. Purpose and scope

L7 turns the forces, the integrators, the variational equations (L3) and the measurement models (L6) into **fits**: the whole force model carried by the `dyn::Force` registry (step 1), batch least squares with the normal equations **scaled by default** (step 2), Levenberg–Marquardt (step 3), a priori constraints, isotropic and RTN (step 4), and the **joint covariance** over the state and every registered parameter (step 5). The exit gate (plan §3.8, amended 2026-09-24): *a fit over real data reaches its frozen residual and reports a joint covariance whose correlations are reproduced by finite differences*; cannonball fits with L4's forces reach the `G-*` frozen residuals with their force parameters stated in the test, and a box-wing fit reading L5's library reaches them too.

**Not in scope:** the campaigns (L8); a sail's own macromodel (§3.9); station displacement (outside the MVP, `SPEC-measmod`); the NRLMSISE-00 altitude cutoffs and the attitude discontinuities as integrator events are **carried into** this layer (plan §3.8) and are decided at the step that fits an arc through them.

**Stubs, and when each is written:** §3 (definitions) and §4 (required behaviour) per step, as each step opens, after its rule-4 report is ruled on; §5 (interfaces), §6 (precision), §7 (refusals) likewise; §9 (provenance) at each close. *(v0.2.)* **Step 1 is not specified here.** Ruling R1 (2026-10-06) puts the force-model plugins in a module of their own, `forcemodel` (`SPEC-forcemodel.md`, Spec ID `FMOD`), because the estimator of steps 2 – 5 depends only on `dyn`'s `Force` interface and not on `gravity`, `tides`, `thirdbody` or `relativity`; this document's §3 – §7 are written for steps 2 – 5 when each opens, and its §8.1 is the exit gate's, which spans all five.

## 3. Definitions and conventions

*(Step 2, v0.3.)* The exit gate's statistic is defined in §8.1 because that is where it is registered. What follows is the batch estimator of step 2. **Derivation declaration:** written from `DEM89`, `LAPK`, `NISTL`, `GEO1`, `BRN52`, `MINP` and from no implementation of any estimator; the predecessor's code was not opened.

**3.1 The problem.** A linearised weighted least-squares problem over `n` named **parameters**: `m` scalar **observation rows** `(aₖ, rₖ, σₖ)`, `aₖ ∈ ℝⁿ` the partials of the modelled observation with respect to the parameters at the linearisation point, `rₖ` the **residual** (observed minus modelled) and `σₖ > 0` its standard deviation, in the residual's own units. The estimator returns the **correction** `x̂` that minimises `Ω(x) = Σₖ ((rₖ − aₖᵀx)/σₖ)²` (adding it to the linearisation point is the caller's). Rows are **whitened** on entry: `ãₖ = fl(aₖ/σₖ)` componentwise, `r̃ₖ = fl(rₖ/σₖ)` (σₖ = 1 divides exactly). **The whitened doubles are the data**: every bound below is relative to the exact solution of the problem whose rows are those doubles.

**3.2 The normal equations.** `N = Σₖ ãₖãₖᵀ`, `b = Σₖ ãₖr̃ₖ`, formed as `EST-R-203` states. `D = diag(√Nᵢᵢ)`, `A = D⁻¹ND⁻¹` (unit diagonal, the matrix of the cosines between the whitened columns). **The scaled error** of a computed solution `x̂` against the exact `x = N⁻¹b` is `φ(x̂, x) = ‖D(x̂ − x)‖₂ / ‖Dx̂‖₂`: **invariant under any change of the parameters' units** (a positive diagonal rescaling of the columns changes `D` and `x` inversely), which is the reason it is the measure of this specification and a relative error of the unscaled vector is not (`DEM89`: small components of `x` are computed to the same relative accuracy as large ones when `κ(A)` is moderate).

**3.3 The scaling (the manager's ruling R6).** For a free parameter `i`, `Nᵢᵢ = m·2ᵏ` with `m ∈ [½, 1)` (`frexp`), the **scale exponent** is `eᵢ = ⌊k/2⌋` (`EST-R-201`), `S = diag(2^(−eᵢ))`, `N_s = S N S`, `b_s = S b`, `y = S⁻¹x`, `Q_s = N_s⁻¹`, `Q = N⁻¹ = S Q_s S`. **Scaling by exact powers of two is exact** (barring over- and underflow, `LAPK`): `N_s` carries no rounding the scaling added, and the scaled diagonal `Nᵢᵢ 2^(−2eᵢ)` lies in `[½, 2)`, so `κ₂(N_s) ≤ 4 κ₂(D⁻¹ND⁻¹)` and `N_s` is within `4n` of the best diagonal scaling (`DEM89`'s statement of Van der Sluis). **Consequence, stated so that nobody expects otherwise** (the manager's correction of the premise of this step, 2026-10-06): the scaling cannot change the Cholesky solution — `DEM89` Theorem 2.1 proves that the unscaled factorisation already meets the scaled bound, and with powers of two the two are *bit for bit the same computation* (measured on the eleven NIST datasets, PROVENANCE §40.7). What it changes is **every decision read from the matrix**: the condition number, the observability, the refusals, the per-parameter diagnostics, the correlations, and (step 3) the damping and the tolerances — each of which moves by orders of magnitude with a parameter's unit when read from the unscaled matrix and does not when read from `N_s`.

**3.4 The error model.** `u = 2⁻⁵³`, the unit roundoff of IEEE binary64 under round-to-nearest; `γₖ = ku/(1 − ku)`; `χₙ = 1/(1 − (n+1)u)`. The constants below are `DEM89`'s: Lemma 2.1 (`|(L̂L̂ᵀ − H)ᵢⱼ| ≤ (n+1)u χₙ √(HᵢᵢHⱼⱼ)`), the substitutions' `|δL| ≤ nu|L̂|` that its proof uses, and the proof's final line, `‖D⁻¹FD⁻¹‖₂ ≤ nη + χₙ[(3n²+n)u + n³u²]`, `η` the relative size of the initial error of the matrix factored. For the forming of `N` the elementary dot-product bound `|fl(Σₖ xₖyₖ) − Σₖ xₖyₖ| ≤ γₘ Σₖ|xₖyₖ|` and Cauchy–Schwarz (`Σₖ|ãₖᵢãₖⱼ| ≤ √(NᵢᵢNⱼⱼ)`) give a **componentwise-scaled** error, `|N̂ᵢⱼ − Nᵢⱼ| ≤ γₘ√(NᵢᵢNⱼⱼ)` and `|b̂ᵢ − bᵢ| ≤ γₘ√Nᵢᵢ‖r̃‖₂`, which is exactly the form `DEM89` handles. Hence
- **`Θ_s(n) = χₙ [ (3n² + n) u + n³u² ]`** — the factorisation and the two substitutions, in the scaled 2-norm;
- **`Θ_N(n, m) = n γₘ (1 + γₘ) + Θ_s(n)`** — plus the forming of `N`; the test-level versions add `n·η_rep`, `η_rep` the relative representation error of decimal data rounded to double (`2.01 u`, §6.1).
`n` is the number of FREE parameters and `m` the number of rows. **The solve's certified relative error** is `B` of `EST-R-206`.

**3.5 What a solution reports** (`EST-R-207`, `-210`): the condition of `N_s` (an estimate from below and a bound from above), each parameter's own `κᵢ = Nᵢᵢ(N⁻¹)ᵢᵢ = (N_s)ᵢᵢ(Q_s)ᵢᵢ` (unit-free: `GEO1` §10.5), the **weakest direction**, `B`, the residual sum of squares `Ω̂`, the degrees of freedom `ν = m − n_free`, the variance of unit weight `σ̂₀² = Ω̂/ν`, and the covariance **both ways** — formal (`σ₀ = 1`: the weights as stated) and scaled (`σ̂₀²`) — never one for the other (`BRN52` §7.2).

**3.6 The nonlinear problem (step 3, v0.4).** **Derivation declaration:** written from `DTU`, `NIE99`, `MINP` and `NISTN`, and from no implementation of any least-squares code (the MINPACK listings were never opened). The module minimises `F(x) = ½ Σₖ rₖ(x)²`, `rₖ = (yₖ − fₖ(x))/σₖ` **whitened by the caller** (a weight is never defaulted, as in `EST-R-202`); `A = ∂f/∂x` divided row by row by `σₖ` is the Jacobian **of the model**, so that the Gauss–Newton step solves `N h = b` with `N = AᵀA` and `b = Aᵀr` and moves `x` towards the data (the sign convention of §3.1: *residual = observed − modelled*). The model is a callable returning, at a point `x`, the residuals, the Jacobian (only when asked) and optionally the objective (an objective returned by the model replaces `½Σr²`: step 4's prior is part of the objective, and the gain ratio is taken on the **whole** objective), **or a refusal**. **The damped system, in the scaled variables:** at each iterate the scale exponents `eᵢ` of `EST-R-201` are recomputed from the diagonal of that iterate's `N`; the damped step solves `(N + μW)h = b` with `W = diag(2^(2eᵢ))`, which is `(N_s + μI)y = b_s` in the scaled variables, **through step 2's `from_normal_system`**: the scaled path stays the **only** path and step 3 writes no factorisation. **The gain ratio** is `ρ = (F(x) − F(x+h)) / L`, `L = ½(hᵀb + μ hᵀWh)`. **The gradient cosine** is `c = maxᵢ |cos(r, aᵢ)|` over the columns `aᵢ` of `A`: a dimensionless measure of how far `Aᵀr` is from zero, which does not change when the residuals or a parameter's unit are rescaled. **A statement that holds throughout:** every decision step 3 reads from the matrix (the scale, the damping, the tests) is made on the scaled quantities and is dimensionless (§3.3's consequence).

**3.7 The a priori constraint (step 4, v0.5, 2026-11-27).** **Derivation declaration:** written from `BRN52` §7.5, `GEO1` §10.1 – 10.2 and `FRAME-R-040` – `-044`, read from the pinned text layers, and from no implementation of any constraint (none of step 4 exists; the manager's rulings R13 and R14 of 2026-10-06 are in `plan/subplan_L7/L7-4.md`). **A constraint is information about the parameters that the data did not give**, entered as a Gaussian prior with centre `x_A` and information matrix `Λ` (`Λ = V_A⁻¹` of `GEO1`; `HᵀP_hH` of `BRN52`, with `H` a selection of the parameters). It is read, as `BRN52` reads it, as **pseudo-observations**: `r` rows `(L, d)` with `LᵀL = Λ`, which enter `N` and `b` exactly as data rows do (`EST-R-203`) and therefore **before the scaling of `EST-R-201`** — the scale exponents are taken from the diagonal of the **total** `N = Σ ãₖãₖᵀ + Λ`, so that a tight constraint is scaled away instead of ruining the conditioning (§3.3: the scaling is exact and decides only what is read from the matrix).

**Variables.** In the batch problem of §3.1 the unknown is the correction `c` to a linearisation point `x_lin`; the prior's **departure** is `d = fl(x_A − x_lin)` (the prior's residual at the linearisation point: `GEO1`'s `x_A − x̂⁽ⁿ⁾`). **The objective the estimator minimises is**

**`F_tot(c) = ½‖r̃ − Ac‖² + ½ (c − d)ᵀ Λ (c − d)`,**

the data's `½Ω` plus the prior's quadratic form in the **absolute** variable (`x = x_lin + c`: `(x − x_A) = (c − d)`). Its normal equations are `(N + Λ)c = b + Λd`, the sum `BRN52` prints with `HᵀP_hh` equal to `Λd` — and `GEO1`'s iteration, the term `Σ_A⁻¹(x_A − x̂⁽ⁿ⁾)` being `Λd` at the iterate `x̂⁽ⁿ⁾ = x_lin`. **In the nonlinear problem of §3.6**, at the current iterate `x` the model's rows are the data's and the prior's rows are `(L, L(x_A − x))`: the gradient right-hand side is `b_tot = b_data + Λ(x_A − x)` and **the objective the gain ratio `ρ` sees is the whole `F_tot(x) = ½‖r(x)‖² + ½(x − x_A)ᵀΛ(x − x_A)`**, so that a step which lowers the data's misfit and raises the prior's by more is *rejected*.

**The two forms of `Λ`.**
- **Isotropic.** Over the named parameters of a block, `Λ = diag(σᵢ⁻²)`; **`I/σ²`** is the case of one `σ` for the block. Row `i` of `L` is `eᵢ/σᵢ`.
- **RTN.** A block is **three named parameters that are the inertial Cartesian components of one vector** — a position, a velocity, or any vector-valued force parameter — **each in its own block and its own fixed basis**. With `B` the 3 × 3 matrix `rtn_basis(r_A, v_A)` returns (**its rows are `ê_R`, `ê_T`, `ê_N`: `FRAME-R-040`; it maps inertial components to RTN components**),
  **`Λ_block = Bᵀ diag(σ_R⁻², σ_T⁻², σ_N⁻²) B`**, i.e. `(c − d)ᵀΛ(c − d) = Σ_k ((B(c − d))_k / σ_k)²` over `k = R, T, N`. *(The ruled formula is written `R diag(σ⁻²) Rᵀ` with `R` the matrix whose **columns** are the axes: `R = Bᵀ`. The two are the same matrix; **a transposed basis is the error this note exists to prevent**, and `EST-A-403` and `EST-A-407` each carry a control for it.)* Row `k` of `L` is `(1/σ_k)·B_k` on the block's three parameters. **`B` is computed once, from the a priori state `(r_A, v_A)`, and held fixed through the iteration**: it is a statement about the prior (where its axes lie), not a nonlinear constraint, so no derivative of `B` enters a Jacobian. `ê_T` is **not** the velocity direction (`FRAME-R-042`) and a velocity block uses the position's basis frozen at the epoch (`FRAME-R-043`); a rectilinear or zero state has no basis and `rtn_basis` refuses it (`FRAME-R-044`, `FRAME-F-004`): the caller has then **no prior to give**.
- **A hard fix is elimination (`EST-R-209`), never `σ → 0`.** `σ → 0` is accepted, to the range of `EST-F-402`, and `EST-A-402` measures how nearly it reproduces the elimination; it is **not** how a parameter is fixed.

**What a prior changes in what a solution reports** (`EST-R-403`): the residual sum of squares has two parts, the data's `Ω̂_data = Σₖ ρ̂ₖ²` and the prior's `Ω̂_prior = Σ (Lĉ − d_s)ₖ²` (its rows' residuals), `Ω̂_tot` their sum; the prior rows are counted as observations, `ν = m + r − n_free`, `r` the number of prior rows. **This counting is the definition adopted here and `EST-Q-007` asks the manager to rule on it**: `BRN52` §7.5 prints the NEQ and calls the constraints pseudo-observations but, in the sections searched (`PROVENANCE.md` §40.10), prints a degrees-of-freedom rule for the data alone (`f = n − u`, §7.2). The posterior cofactor is `Q_post = (N + Λ)⁻¹`, which is what the solve's cofactor already is (`EST-R-205`).

**3.8 The joint covariance and the two-parameter confidence regions (step 5, v0.6, 2026-11-27).** **Derivation declaration:** written from `BRN52` §7.2 and §8.3.2, `GEO1` §10.5 and §11.2, `MINP` §2.8, `NISTL`, `AND10` §2.5.2 and the closed forms of `EHB`, and from no implementation (none of step 5 exists; the manager's ruling R15 of 2026-10-06 is in `plan/subplan_L7/L7-5.md`). **Nothing new is solved:** step 5 reads what step 2's `Solution` already holds (`EST-R-205`, `-207`, `-210`) — the cofactor `Q` in the parameters' units (the scaled inverse `Q_s`, unscaled exactly by `EST-R-201`), the scaled cofactor `Q_s`, `Ω̂` and `ν` — and **adds no factorisation**; `modules/estimation` stays `core`-only (`EST-Q-008`).

**The joint parameter vector.** The parameters are, in this order, **the orbital state** (position then velocity: **6** parameters, the caller's names) **followed by the force parameters in the registration order of L3's `ParameterRegistry`** (the order in which `declare` issued them, which is the order of the sensitivity columns of `propagate_with_sensitivities`, `DYN-R-005`), each under the name its declaration carries. `Solution::names` **must equal** that list element for element (`EST-R-503`); a different order or set is refused (`EST-F-505`), because a covariance whose rows are labelled in another order than the sensitivity columns is a wrong covariance with right-looking numbers. The **correlation matrix** is `ρᵢⱼ = Q_s,ᵢⱼ/√(Q_s,ᵢᵢ Q_s,ⱼⱼ)` from the **scaled** cofactor (`EST-R-210`; unit-free by construction, clamped to `[−1, 1]`, `ρᵢᵢ = 1`).

**Formal and scaled, never one for the other** (`BRN52` §7.2; `EST-R-210`). The **formal** covariance is `Q` (`σ₀ = 1`: the weights as stated) and is the covariance of the mode "**known `σ₀`**"; the **scaled** covariance is `σ̂₀²Q`, `σ̂₀² = Ω̂/ν`, and is the covariance of the mode "**estimated `σ₀`**". A report carries both side by side; a function never takes a default mode and never falls back from one to the other (`EST-R-505`).

**The two-parameter marginal region.** For a pair `(i, j)`, `i ≠ j`, both free, with `θ̂_ij` the two estimates and `C_ij` the 2 × 2 block of the covariance **of the mode**, the region is **`(θ_ij − θ̂_ij)ᵀ C_ij⁻¹ (θ_ij − θ̂_ij) ≤ c²`**, where
- **known `σ₀`:** `C_ij = σ₀² Q_ij` (the formal block when `σ₀ = 1`, the weights as stated — the registered case; a stated `σ₀` is a finite positive number the caller gives) and **`c² = χ²₂(p) = −2 ln(1 − p)`**, `p ∈ (0, 1)`;
- **estimated `σ̂₀`:** `C_ij = σ̂₀² Q_ij` and **`c² = 2F_p(2, ν) = ν((1 − p)^(−2/ν) − 1)`**, `ν ≥ 1` (the upper critical value `F_α(2, ν) = (ν/2)(α^(−2/ν) − 1)` at `α = 1 − p`, times 2).
Where the covariance is the `Q` of a *linear* fit the region is **exact**: for a linear model `Ω(θ) = Ω̂ + (θ − θ̂)ᵀN(θ − θ̂)`, and minimising out every other parameter leaves **`Ω(θ_i, θ_j) − Ω̂ = (θ_ij − θ̂_ij)ᵀ (Q_ij)⁻¹ (θ_ij − θ̂_ij)`** (the Schur complement of `N` is `Q_ij⁻¹`): the profile of the objective is the ellipse's own quadratic form. **That identity is the independent evidence** (ruling R15): the profile is computed **from the rows**, by a different algorithm (Householder QR on the problem with the two parameters held), never from `Q`. For a nonlinear fit the region is the first-order one and **no claim is made beyond it** (`EST-R-506`).

**What the numbers mean — said once because the sources invite the slip.** `c² = 1` is **not** the 68.27 % region: in two dimensions the contour `c² = 1` contains `1 − e^(−½) = 39.35 %` (`AND10` §2.5.2 prints 39.4 %), and the 68.27 % region is `c² = −2 ln(1 − 0.682689492137086) = 2.2957489…`. The level `p` is a *coverage probability of the region*, not a number of σ.

**3.9 The exit gate's fit (v0.7, 2026-11-27; registered before any exit-gate code, plan §4 rule 3).** **Derivation declaration:** written from §8.1 (v0.1 – v0.2, kept), `plan/PLAN.md` §3.8, `L7.REPORT.md` §A.5 (the manager's decisions on the gravity degree, the planets and `extrapolate_secular`), `oracle/ORACLE.md` §6 and `oracle/cases.tsv`, the headers of `odl/srp/`, `odl/srp_analytic/`, `odl/macromodel/`, `odl/spacecraft/`, `odl/forcemodel/`, `odl/dynamics/` and `odl/io/sp3.hpp`, and the pinned SP3 headers — all read for this registration (`PROVENANCE.md` §40.12), **no fit run**. **What is registered here is the configuration and its checks; the numbers of the first fit are not known to anyone.**

**The three rows and their files.** `G-01` = **G01 on the file `igs-final-sp3-2023-022`** (`IGS0OPSFIN_20230220000_01D_15M_ORB.SP3`, 2023-01-22), `G-02` = **G01 on `igs-final-sp3-2023-023`** (2023-01-23), `G-03` = **G05 on `-022`**. Each file, as its header says: `#cP2023 1 22 0 0 0.00000000 96 ORBIT IGS20 HLM IGS` (`-023`: `1 23`), **96 epochs** at **900 s** from GPS time 0 s (`-023`: 86 400 s) of GPS week 2246, **31 satellites** with `G01` and `G05` among them; **the header flag is `P`: positions only, no velocities**; time system GPS; reference frame IGS20. In both files **`G01` and `G05` have 96 position records each and none carries the bad-or-absent value `999999`** (`grep -c` of the satellites' own lines: 96, 96 and 0, in each file). The last epoch of a file is 23:45 (85 500 s after its first), so **the arc is the file's 96 epochs, not 24 h**.

**The fit.** Seven parameters in the registry's order, `[x, y, z, vx, vy, vz, srp_scale]`: the GCRS state **at the arc's middle epoch** and the SRP scale (`EST-R-901`). **`EST-Q-006` (decided 2026-10-06: the middle epoch, as NIST's own remedy for Filip is centring) is applied: the state is estimated at 43 200 s of the file (epoch index 48, the 12:00:00 epoch of the file's grid), and each side is propagated from there — backwards for the first 48 epochs**, which the integrator supports (`variational.cpp` takes `seconds` of either sign and gives the first step the sign of `seconds`). **Observations:** the three position components of every one of the 96 epochs, residual **`pᵢ − qᵢ(θ)` in the ITRS, in metres** (the SP3 file's kilometres × 10³, the one named crossing), **unit weight (`σ = 1 m` for every row)**, so that the quantity the fit minimises, `Ω = Σᵢ ‖qᵢ − pᵢ‖²`, is exactly `N·S_N²` of §8.1 and `S_N = √(Ω/N)`; rotating the residual into the GCRS by the (orthogonal) chain changes no norm, so the rows are formed in whichever frame the code finds convenient and **`S` is always the ITRS one**. **The start:** the file carries no velocities, so the starting state is **the position of epoch 48 and the velocity of the degree-8 Lagrange polynomial through the nine epochs 44 – 52, differentiated at epoch 48** (each component in the ITRS, then carried to the GCRS by the chain with the Earth-rotation term), and `srp_scale = 1`; **a second, perturbed start** (the first plus 1 km along each position axis and 1 m/s along each velocity axis, `srp_scale = 2`) is run for each row and **reported, not gated** — a control that the minimum does not depend on the start.

**The forces (§A.5, as ruled).** **Cannonball fits:** `Gravity` (EGM2008) **degree 10, order 10** with `extrapolate_secular = true` (the size of the secular-pole shift it moves is **printed** in each fit's record, `secular_pole=extrapolated`); **set A — `ThirdBody` Sun and Moon, `Tides` (the solid Earth tide and the solid pole tide, the FES2004 ocean tide at degree 7 — `TN36-6` Table 6.1's value at GPS — and, **if the Committee wants it, Desai's ocean pole tide** (`EST-Q-011`)), and the three `Relativity` terms (Schwarzschild, Lense–Thirring, de Sitter)**; and the SRP of the cannonball — `Srp` over a `Macromodel` of one `SphericalSurface` — carried by the seventh parameter through the decorator of `EST-R-901`. **Printed beside every fit, replacing nothing:** the same fit at gravity **degree 12** (`TN36-6` Table 6.1) and with **set B's planets** added (the five planets of `SPEC-forcemodel` `FMOD-A-001`). **The box-wing fits** (the stronger claim): the same forces with `Srp` reading **`gps_block_iif()` for G01 (SVN63) and `gps_block_iir_m()` for G05 (SVN50)** and the attitude law L4 step 6 gates, the SRP scale carried by the same decorator. **The cannonball's area and mass are stated in the test** (plan §3.8): `EST-Q-011`.

**What a converged fit is expected to say.** The model is not the data's: the IGS final orbit is a weighted mean of eight analysis centres' fits with the ECOM empirical model, the fit here has one SRP scale and no empirical accelerations, and SP3 positions carry centimetres of their own noise. So **the registered expectation is that the iteration ends `stalled`, `converged_step` or `converged_gradient` — never `refused` and never `max_iterations` — with a finite `S` of the order of decimetres to centimetres**, the floor being the model's mismatch (the manner of step 3's `ARC-X-014`, where `stalled` at the noise floor was the truthful end state); `S` is **not** zero and **not** expected to equal `F`. The statuses are reported as step 3 reports them. **The gross-error control of the iteration:** the final `S_N` is below `S_N` at the start by a factor of at least `10` (a fit that does not reduce the starting residual tenfold has not fitted).

## 4. Required behaviour

*(Written per step; step 2's requirements are `EST-R-201` – `EST-R-213`, then the exit gate's.)*

**Step 2 — the batch estimator, the normal equations scaled by default.**

- **EST-R-201.** **The scale rule, and no path without it.** For a free parameter whose diagonal `d = N̂ᵢᵢ` is finite and lies in `[2⁻¹⁰⁰⁰, 2¹⁰⁰⁰]`, write `d = m·2ᵏ` with `m ∈ [½, 1)` (`std::frexp`); **the scale exponent is `e = ⌊k/2⌋`** (an integer floor division, no logarithm and no tie to break); the scaled diagonal `d·2^(−2e)` lies in `[½, 2)`. `N_s,ᵢⱼ = ldexp(N̂ᵢⱼ, −(eᵢ+eⱼ))`, `b_s,ᵢ = ldexp(b̂ᵢ, −eᵢ)`, the solution `xᵢ = ldexp(yᵢ, −eᵢ)` and the cofactor `Qᵢⱼ = ldexp(Q_s,ᵢⱼ, −(eᵢ+eⱼ))` — **all exact**. The interface offers **no option and no function that factors or solves an unscaled matrix**: unscaled quantities are outputs only. A diagonal `≤ 0` is `EST-F-201`; one non-finite or outside the range, `EST-F-206`.
- **EST-R-202.** **Rows.** `add(row, residual, sigma)` requires `row.size() = n`, every value finite and `sigma` finite and `> 0` (`EST-F-204`; **a weight is never defaulted**: the caller states `σ`, even 1). The row and the residual are whitened by division (`fl(aₖ/σₖ)`, `fl(rₖ/σₖ)`) and stored in the order added.
- **EST-R-203.** **The forming.** `N̂ᵢⱼ` (`i ≥ j`) is the plain double sum `(((0 + p₁) + p₂) + …) + p_m`, `pₖ = fl(ãₖᵢ ãₖⱼ)`, **rows in the order added**; the upper triangle is its mirror image; `b̂ᵢ` likewise with `pₖ = fl(ãₖᵢ r̃ₖ)`. The module is built with fused multiply-add contraction **off**, so that this order and these roundings are the registered ones; the bounds of §3.4 hold with or without contraction.
- **EST-R-204.** **The factorisation and the solves** are `DEM89`'s Algorithm 2.1 on the lower triangle of `N_s` and the two substitutions, in plain double: for `i = 0 … n_f−1`, `t = N_s,ᵢᵢ` minus `Σ_{k<i} L²ᵢₖ` **by successive subtraction in the order `k = 0 … i−1`**; **if `!(t > 0)` the factorisation breaks down at `i`** (`EST-F-202`); `Lᵢᵢ = √t`; for `j > i`, `Lⱼᵢ = (N_s,ⱼᵢ minus Σ_{k<i} LⱼₖLᵢₖ by successive subtraction)/Lᵢᵢ`. Forward substitution `Lz = b_s` ascending, back substitution `Lᵀy = z` descending, each by successive subtraction. Extended precision is **not** used in production.
- **EST-R-205.** **The solution and the cofactor.** `x` by the unscaling of `y`. The cofactor by `n_f` solves `N_s q = eⱼ` with the same factor; `Q_s,ᵢⱼ = qⱼ[i]` for `i ≥ j` and the upper triangle its mirror image, so **`Q` is exactly symmetric**. An eliminated parameter has no uncertainty in this model: its row and column of `Q` are exactly 0.
- **EST-R-206.** **What the solve certifies, and the refusal that follows from it.** Let `q₁ = ‖Q̂_s‖₁` (the largest column sum of absolute values of the computed scaled cofactor), `X = (Σᵢ N_s,ᵢᵢ yᵢ²)^½ = ‖Dx̂‖₂`, `ρ̄ = ‖r̃′‖₂` (the residual vector that formed `b̂`, after any elimination), `ρ̄₀ = ‖r̃‖₂` (before), and `μ_E = ‖(Σ_e |ãₖₑ||vₑ|)ₖ‖₂` (0 when nothing is eliminated). **The certified scaled relative error is**
  **`B = 4 q₁ [ Θ_N(n_f, m) + √n_f (γₘ ρ̄ + γ_{2n_e}(ρ̄₀ + μ_E)) / X ]`** — in system mode (`EST-R-213`) the forming terms are absent and `Θ_N` is replaced by `Θ_s(n_f)`; `B = 0` when `b̂ = 0` (then `x̂ = 0` exactly). *Derivation (informative):* `x̂ − x = N⁻¹(Δb − ΔN x̂)` is an identity for the computed `x̂`; `‖D⁻¹ΔND⁻¹‖₂ ≤ Θ_N` and `‖D⁻¹Δb‖₂ ≤ √n_f(…)` by §3.4; `D(x̂ − x) = A⁻¹D⁻¹(Δb − ΔNx̂)`; `‖A⁻¹‖₂ ≤ 2‖N_s⁻¹‖₂` because `N_s` has its diagonal below 2, and `‖N_s⁻¹‖₂ ≤ 2‖Q̂_s‖₂ ≤ 2q₁` whenever `B < ½` (the computed inverse is the inverse of a matrix within `2Θ_N` of `N_s`, and `2Θ_N q₁ < ¼`). **If `B ≥ ½`, or if `Q̂_s` is not finite, the solve certifies not even one digit and is REFUSED (`EST-F-203`)** — a bound, not a number anyone chose.
- **EST-R-207.** **The condition report.** `κ_est = λ_N λ_Q`, the Rayleigh quotients after **100 power iterations** on `N_s` and on `Q_s`, each started from `(1, …, 1)/√n_f` (so `κ_est ≤ κ₂(N_s)`); `κ_up = ‖N_s‖₁ ‖Q_s‖₁` (so `κ₂(N_s) ≤ κ_up`); **`κᵢ = (N_s)ᵢᵢ (Q_s)ᵢᵢ`** for every free parameter (`NaN` for an eliminated one); **the weakest direction** = the unit vector of the `Q_s` iteration, sign fixed so that its largest-magnitude component is positive, in the scaled variables, 0 for eliminated parameters. These are reported with every solution and with `EST-F-203`.
- **EST-R-208.** **Every refusal names the parameter and why.** `EST-F-201` names every parameter with no information. `EST-F-202` names the parameter `j` at which the factorisation broke down, the pivot `t`, and **the dependency**: with `z` the solution of `N_s[0:j−1, 0:j−1] z = N_s[0:j−1, j]` (the same successive-subtraction substitutions on the factor built so far), `N_s[:, j] ≈ Σ_{k<j} z_k N_s[:, k]` — the three parameters of largest `|z_k|` by name with their `z_k`; the weakest direction reported is `(−z, 1)` normalised, in the scaled variables, 0 for the parameters after `j`. `EST-F-203` carries `B`, `κ_est`, `κ_up`, the three largest `κᵢ` by name and the three largest members of the weakest direction by name.
- **EST-R-209.** **The one hatch: elimination of named parameters at stated values, logged.** `SolveOptions::eliminate` lists parameters by name with a value; each name must be known and listed once, each value finite, at least one parameter must remain and the estimator must hold the rows (`EST-F-204`). The elimination is at the row level: `r̃′ₖ = r̃ₖ − ãₖ,e₁ v₁ − ãₖ,e₂ v₂ − …`, left to right in ascending parameter index, each product and each difference rounded; the normal equations are those of the free parameters and `r̃′`; the solution holds every eliminated parameter at its stated value; `ν = m − n_free`; the provenance records them (`EST-R-212`). This is **the operation a hard constraint is** (step 4), and it removes a parameter from the model: it never returns numbers for combinations the data cannot see.
- **EST-R-210.** **The residual, the degrees of freedom and the covariance.** `ρ̂ₖ = fl(r̃ₖ − sₖ)`, `sₖ = Σᵢ ãₖᵢ xᵢ` over **all** parameters in index order (the eliminated at their values) by successive addition; `Ω̂ = Σₖ ρ̂ₖ²` by successive addition; `ν = m − n_free`; `σ̂₀² = Ω̂/ν` for `ν > 0`. **Formal covariance** `= Q` (`σ₀ = 1`, the weights as stated); **scaled covariance** `= σ̂₀² Q`; standard deviations `√Qᵢᵢ` and `σ̂₀√Qᵢᵢ`; **correlation** `ρᵢⱼ = Q_s,ᵢⱼ / √(Q_s,ᵢᵢ Q_s,ⱼⱼ)` computed from the SCALED cofactor (so it is unit-free by construction), clamped to `[−1, 1]`, `ρᵢᵢ = 1`. `σ̂₀² = 0` (a perfect fit) gives an exactly zero scaled covariance while the formal one stays defined. The two covariances are never substituted for each other.
- **EST-R-211.** **Unit invariance, exactly.** If every column `j` of the rows is multiplied by `2^(pⱼ)` (the parameter `xⱼ` is then `2^(−pⱼ)` of what it was), then — barring over- and underflow — the scale exponents move by `pⱼ`, and **`N_s`, `b_s`, the factor, `y`, `Q_s`, `κ_est`, `κ_up`, the `κᵢ`, the weakest direction, the correlations, `B`, `Ω̂`, `σ̂₀²` and every refusal are bit-identical**; `xⱼ` and the cofactor scale by exactly `2^(−pⱼ)` and `2^(−pᵢ−pⱼ)`. (Every operation commutes with exact power-of-two scalings; `frexp`'s exponent moves by `2pⱼ` and `⌊k/2⌋` by `pⱼ`.) For a rescaling that is not a power of two the solutions agree within the sum of the two certified errors (`EST-A-209`).
- **EST-R-212.** **Provenance.** Every solution carries a string in the tree's `key=value;` form, in this key order: `estimation=batch-normal-equations;scaling=powers-of-two;parameters=<n>;free=<n_free>;observations=<m>;eliminated=<name=value,…|none>;exponents=<e₀,e₁,…>;` — the values printed `%.17g`, the exponents those of the free parameters in order.
- **EST-R-213.** **System mode.** `from_normal_system(names, N, b, observations, residual_square_sum)` takes an accumulated normal system (the lower triangle of `N` is read) for callers who hold one — a combination of campaigns, step 4's priors, a published example. The forming terms of `EST-R-206` are absent (the system is exact as given), `Ω̂ = Σr̃² − bᵀx̂` **only when the caller supplied the residual square sum** (a difference of two large numbers, documented as such), and elimination is refused (`EST-F-204`: it needs the rows).
**Step 3 — Levenberg–Marquardt, in the scaled variables** *(v0.4, 2026-11-25; module `estimation`, header `odl/estimation/levenberg_marquardt.hpp`, source `src/levenberg_marquardt.cpp`; the manager's rulings of 2026-10-06 and 2026-10-31, the user's decisions of 2026-11-25 — `plan/subplan_L7/L7-3.md`)*

- **EST-R-301.** **The algorithm.** `DTU`'s Algorithm 3.16 in the scaled variables of §3.6: at every iterate the exponents are recomputed from `N`'s diagonal, the damped system `(N + μW)h = b` is built as `(N_s + μI)y = b_s` and solved by `NormalEquations::from_normal_system`; **no factorisation is written in step 3 and no path solves an unscaled system.** The starting damping is `μ₀ = τ · maxᵢ (N_s)ᵢᵢ` (the scaled diagonal lies in `[½, 2)`, so `τ` multiplies a number of order one).
- **EST-R-302.** **The gain ratio and the damping update.** `ρ = (F(x) − F(x+h)) / L`, `L = ½(hᵀb + μ hᵀWh)`, on the objective the model carries. **A step is taken exactly when `ρ > 0`** (a gain ratio in `(0, ½]` is accepted: `EST-A-311`'s guard of the mutant `ρ > ½`). On acceptance `μ ← μ · max(⅓, 1 − (2ρ − 1)³)` and `ν ← 2` (`DTU` (2.21)); on rejection `μ ← μ · ν` and `ν ← 2ν`, so **consecutive rejections multiply `μ` by 2, 4, 8, …** exactly (`EST-A-313`'s guard of the mutant `ν ← ν`).
- **EST-R-303.** **A failed trial is a rejected step.** A trial the model refuses, a non-finite objective, residual or Jacobian, and a damped system that step 2 refuses all **raise `μ` and the iteration goes on**; none aborts the run. (The model's refusal of a trial is how L6's observability limit — a leg below 3° of elevation, `MEAS-F-010` — reaches the optimiser: Gauss–Newton has no defence against it and the damped method does: §8.3's sparse-arc rows.)
- **EST-R-304.** **The stopping tests are dimensionless.** At every iterate, **in this order**: (a) **the zero-residual test**, `F ≤ residual_fraction · F(x₀)` (10⁻²⁴): `converged_residual` (for the consistent problems whose gradient cosine is indeterminate); (b) **the gradient test**, `c ≤ gradient_cosine` (10⁻¹⁰): `converged_gradient`; (c) **the limit**, `max_iterations` passes (1000, every pass counted, accepted or rejected): `max_iterations`. At every pass, on the damped step **before** its trial: (d) **the step test on the scaled quantities**, `‖h_s‖ ≤ ε₂(‖x_s‖ + ε₂)` with `ε₂ = step_tolerance` (10⁻¹²): `converged_step` **if** `c ≤ stalled_cosine` (10⁻⁴), otherwise **`stalled`** (the step test takes the step that was *not* made: the returned iterate is the last accepted one). Rescaling the residuals or a parameter's unit by powers of two changes none of them (`EST-R-308`).
- **EST-R-305.** **A result carries a status, never a bare "converged"**, with the message naming what ended the run and the numbers: `converged_gradient`, `converged_residual`, `converged_step` (the step test held and the gradient cosine is not above `stalled_cosine`), `stalled` (the step test held while the gradient cosine **is** above it: *the iteration stopped moving without having arrived*, in the test's own sense), `max_iterations`, `refused` (`EST-R-307`). **`stalled` at `χ²/obs ≈ 1` on noisy data is the truthful end state** (§8.3's `ARC-X-014` row: the objective sits on the model's own evaluation-noise floor there, and the user's decision of 2026-11-25 keeps the rule unchanged). The result also carries the last accepted iterate `x`, `F`, the cosine, the pass, accepted and rejected counts, the final `μ`, one history record per pass (`k`, `F` before the trial, the cosine, `μ` used, `ρ` — NaN for a trial that could not be evaluated —, the scaled step, whether accepted), `x` after each accepted step, and **the undamped solve at the last iterate** (step 2's `Solution`: the cofactor, the condition report, `σ̂₀²`) **when step 2 accepts the system**.
- **EST-R-306.** **The defaults** (`LmOptions`), each with where it comes from: `tau = 10⁻³` (registered before the first run; **the candidate τ = 1 lost the held-out test** of `EST-A-331` and the default stays); `gradient_cosine = 10⁻¹⁰`; `residual_fraction = 10⁻²⁴` (**first registered 10⁻³⁰, which sits at the rounding floor of `F` and reported success as `stalled`; changed by the manager's ruling of 2026-10-31, registered before the re-run**); `step_tolerance = 10⁻¹²`; `stalled_cosine = 10⁻⁴`; `max_iterations = 1000`; `mu_max = 10¹⁶`. **No default was tuned to a result seen** (§8.3's rows state what each decision rested on).
- **EST-R-307.** **`refused`** means: the model refused **the starting point** (`EST-F-301`; no pass was made); or `μ` exceeded `mu_max` (`EST-F-302`); or a stop (the step test or the limit) came **while the latest trial could not be evaluated** (`EST-F-303`) — the run never reports arrival at a point where nothing was evaluated. *(The narrowing of `refused` to the third case alone, and a step test in the Hessian's metric, were proposed in commit `2033ad8` of `plan/subplan_L7/L7-3.md`, suspended on 2026-10-31 and **retired on 2026-11-25 without ever being built** (Amendment 9): `refused` and the stopping rule stand as stated here.)*
- **EST-R-308.** **Invariance under power-of-two rescaling, exactly.** If the residuals are multiplied by `2^q` and the parameters' units by `2^(pⱼ)`, the iterates (rescaled), the statuses, the damping and every decision are **bit-identical** (`MINP` §2.5's definition of scale invariance). **No such claim is made for any other change of variables**: Meyer's two formulations (`DTU` Example 3.19) are *not* related by a power of two and their iterates differ (up to 0.1685 relative over 152 common steps, `EST-A-320`).
- **EST-R-309.** **Stated limits, not claims.** (i) The module makes **no claim that the scaled algorithm is more robust than `DTU`'s unscaled one**: it reaches NIST's `F` from 46 of 54 starts against the unscaled control's 51 (§8.3), and its advantage is `EST-R-308`, not reach; (ii) **no claim about Levenberg–Marquardt's robustness is made from the dense sail arc**, on which plain Gauss–Newton also reaches the truth from every evaluable start: that problem is close to linear (`ARC-L-001`, §8.3); the sparse arc is where the damped method has its evidence; (iii) the integrator's evaluation noise sets a floor under `F` and the gradient cosine of a model evaluated through `dyn::propagate` (`ARC-X-014`): a fit cannot be driven below it and the status says so (`EST-R-305`).
**Step 4 — the a priori constraints, isotropic and RTN** *(v0.5, 2026-11-27, **registered before any line of step 4 exists**; module `estimation`, namespace `odl::estimation`, headers `odl/estimation/prior.hpp` (new) and the existing `normal_equations.hpp`; `core`-only; the manager's rulings R13 and R14 of 2026-10-06 — `plan/subplan_L7/L7-4.md`)*

- **EST-R-401.** **A prior block, and what is validated before anything is added.** A block names its parameters and gives, for each, a centre `x_A` and a standard deviation; the **isotropic** block takes any number of parameters, each with its own `σᵢ`; the **RTN** block takes **exactly three** parameters (the inertial `x, y, z` components of one vector, in that order), a 3 × 3 basis in the layout of `FRAME-R-040` (**row-major, rows `ê_R`, `ê_T`, `ê_N`**) and `(σ_R, σ_T, σ_N)`. Validation, **all of it before any row is formed** and with the offending name in the diagnostic (`EST-F-401` – `EST-F-406`): every name known to the estimator and listed once in its block; **no parameter in two blocks** (no silent combination: a caller who wants two pieces of information about one parameter builds one block); every centre and `σ` finite and `σ` in `[2⁻⁵⁰⁰, 2⁵⁰⁰]` (so that `σ⁻²` and every whitened entry stay normal doubles — a range, not a tuned floor); the basis finite and orthonormal, `max |B Bᵀ − I| ≤ 10⁻¹²`; **no parameter that is both constrained and eliminated** (`EST-R-209`).
- **EST-R-402.** **The prior's rows, formed with the data's, before the scaling.** An isotropic block adds, per parameter `i`, the whitened row `(eᵢ/σᵢ)` with residual `d̃ᵢ = fl(dᵢ/σᵢ)`; an RTN block adds three rows, `k = R, T, N`, with entries `fl(B_kj/σ_k)` on its three parameters and residual `fl(fl(Σ_j B_kj dⱼ)/σ_k)`, `Σ_j` in `j = x, y, z` by successive addition; where `d = fl(x_A − x_lin)` is taken at the linearisation point the **caller states with the block** (`add_prior(block, x_lin)`). The rows are appended **after the data's, in the order the blocks were added**, and are formed into `N̂` and `b̂` by the rule of `EST-R-203` (so `Λ` is **exactly symmetric by construction**, the upper triangle the mirror of the lower, and a `NormalEquations` that has no prior is **bit-identical to step 2's**, every field and the provenance string). The exponents of `EST-R-201` are then taken from the diagonal of the **total** `N̂`; **no path adds a prior after the scaling, and none solves without it.**
- **EST-R-403.** **What the solution reports, and the counts.** `Solution` gains the number of prior rows `r`, **`Ω̂_data`, `Ω̂_prior` and `Ω̂_tot = Ω̂_data + Ω̂_prior`** (the data's from the data rows' residuals `ρ̂ₖ` as in `EST-R-210`, the prior's from the prior rows' residuals `fl(d̃ₖ − Σⱼ L̃ₖⱼ xⱼ)`, both by successive addition), `ν = m + r − n_free` (`EST-Q-007`) and `σ̂₀² = Ω̂_tot/ν` for `ν > 0`; the formal covariance `Q = (N + Λ)⁻¹` (**the posterior cofactor**) and the scaled one `σ̂₀²Q` are as in `EST-R-210`. **The provenance string of `EST-R-212` gets, after `exponents=…;` and only when at least one block was added, the keys `priors=<number of blocks>;prior_rows=<r>;`** — a solution with no prior carries the step-2 string byte for byte. The step-2 and step-3 tests are unchanged. *(Clarified 2026-11-27, Amendment 1, confirmed by `odl Committee`: the certified error `B` of `EST-R-206` is evaluated over **all `m + r` rows** of the formed system — the data's `m` and the prior's `r` — in `gamma`, `Theta_N` and the residual norms; the first text did not say so, and the implementation has done so since its first run.)*
- **EST-R-404.** **The damped method sees the whole objective.** `with_priors(model, blocks)` wraps an `LmModel` and returns an `LmModel` whose evaluation at `x` is the model's, **with the prior's rows appended** — residual `L(x_A − x)` and Jacobian `L`, `m + r` rows — so that `F_tot(x) = ½‖r(x)‖² + ½(x − x_A)ᵀΛ(x − x_A)` is the `½Σr²` the gain ratio of `EST-R-302` is taken on, the gradient is `b_tot = b_data + Λ(x_A − x)`, and **`levenberg_marquardt.cpp` is not changed** (the prior reaches it as rows; an objective carried by the model, `LmEvaluation::objective`, is not needed and **is not used**: a model that already carries one is refused, `EST-F-404`, because the prior's half could not be added to it without a second, unregistered convention). Priors and the model share the parameter names of the call; a name the prior holds that the call does not is `EST-F-401`.
- **EST-R-405.** **Exact invariance, and the hard fix.** If a column `j` of the data rows is multiplied by `2^(pⱼ)` — and **the prior's `σⱼ` and centre by the same `2^(pⱼ)`** for an isotropic parameter, or **all three of an RTN block's `σ_k` and `d` by one common `2^p`** (the basis `B` is then unchanged: a different power on one component of a block would rotate the ellipsoid and is **not** a power-of-two rescaling) — then every quantity listed in `EST-R-211` **and `Λ`, the prior rows' sums, `Ω̂_prior`, `Ω̂_tot`, `ν` and the new provenance keys** are bit-identical or scale by exactly the power of two, in the batch solve and, for `EST-R-308`, in every pass of the damped method. **A hard fix is the elimination of `EST-R-209`**: nothing in this step turns `σ → 0` into a hatch, there is no `σ` that means "fixed", and the range of `EST-R-401` is the only limit (`EST-A-402` measures how far the small-`σ` limit agrees with the elimination; it is the evidence that the information form is safe, not an invitation).
- **EST-R-406.** **Observability.** The refusals of step 2 are unchanged and a prior does not hide them: a parameter that no row observes **and that no block constrains** is `EST-F-201` (named), exactly as without step 4; **a parameter that only a block constrains is observable** (its diagonal is `Λᵢᵢ > 0`: `GEO1` §10.2, *"the existence of the a priori information alone guarantees that the inverse of A exists"*) and the solve returns the prior's centre for it, to the certified error `B`.
- **EST-R-407.** **Stated limits, not claims.** (i) The module makes **no claim that a constraint improves an estimate**: it **adds information**, and a wrong centre with a tight `σ` is returned as the answer (that is the bias of `EST-A-407`, registered as a number); (ii) **the evidence of step 4 is identities and one constructed comparison**, because neither `BRN52` nor `GEO1` prints a numerical example of a constraint: the comparison is a **linear Gaussian construction with a known truth** and its predicted values are closed forms; **it makes no claim about any real orbit, any real tracking network or any value of `σ_T` that a user should choose**; (iii) the noise in `EST-A-407`'s replicates is **reported**, and gates nothing (`EST-A-407`: the RTN bias is below the replicates' standard error, and the table says so).
**Step 5 — the joint covariance and the two-parameter confidence regions** *(v0.6, 2026-11-27, **registered before any line of step 5 exists**; module `estimation`, namespace `odl::estimation`, new header `odl/estimation/joint_covariance.hpp`; `core`-only; the manager's ruling R15 of 2026-10-06 — `plan/subplan_L7/L7-5.md`)*

- **EST-R-501.** **The critical values, by their closed forms, and nothing else.** `χ²₂(p) = −2 ln(1 − p)` is computed as **`−2·log1p(−p)`**; `2F_p(2, ν) = ν((1 − p)^(−2/ν) − 1)` as **`ν·expm1(−(2/ν)·log1p(−p))`** (so that neither `1 − p` nor the subtraction of 1 loses digits when `p` is small or `ν` large); `p ∈ (0, 1)` **open**, finite, and `ν ≥ 1` (an integer-valued degrees of freedom; `EST-F-501` otherwise). **No table, no iteration, no inverse-CDF search** is used: the CDFs `1 − e^(−x/2)` and `1 − (1 + 2x/ν)^(−ν/2)` are the *test's* independent route back (`EST-A-501`). `confidence_critical_value(mode, p, ν)` returns `χ²₂(p)` for the mode "known `σ₀`" and `2F_p(2, ν)` for "estimated `σ₀`".
- **EST-R-502.** **The marginal two-parameter ellipse.** For a pair `(name_i, name_j)` of one `Solution`, a mode, a level `p`, and for the known mode the stated `σ₀` (default 1, finite, `> 0`), the builder returns the centre `θ̂_ij`, the covariance block `C_ij` **of the mode** (known: `σ₀²Q_ij`; estimated: `σ̂₀²Q_ij`), the level `c²` of `EST-R-501`, the semi-axes `a ≥ b = √(λ± c²)` with `λ± = ½(C_ii + C_jj) ± √(¼(C_ii − C_jj)² + C_ij²)`, the orientation of the major axis from the `i` axis `θ = ½ atan2(2C_ij, C_ii − C_jj)` (radians, in `(−π/2, π/2]`), and the operations `quadratic(v) = vᵀC⁻¹v` (the 2 × 2 inverse by its adjugate and determinant), `contains(point)` (`quadratic ≤ c²`) and `boundary_point(t) = θ̂ + R(θ)·(a cos t, b sin t)`. **The mode is stated by the caller, always** (`EST-R-505`).
- **EST-R-503.** **The layout: the state, then L3's registration order.** `JointParameterLayout::create(state_names (6), force_names)` holds the six state names and the force parameters' names **in the order L3's `ParameterRegistry` issued them**; it refuses (`EST-F-505`) a state of other than six names, an empty or repeated name. `joint_covariance(Solution, layout)` requires `Solution::names ==` the layout's full list, element for element, and refuses otherwise (`EST-F-505`, naming the first position that differs and both names). The module never sees `dyn::ParameterRegistry` (`core`-only): **a test that links `dyn` and `estimation` builds the layout from the registry's declarations in issue order** (`EST-A-506`).
- **EST-R-504.** **The joint covariance report.** `JointCovariance` carries, for the whole parameter vector in layout order: the formal covariance `Q`; the scaled covariance `σ̂₀²Q` **when `ν > 0` and `Ω̂` is known** (otherwise the scaled part is **absent** and a request for it is `EST-F-503`, never a substitute); the standard deviations `√Qᵢᵢ` (formal) and `σ̂₀√Qᵢᵢ` (scaled); the correlation matrix of `EST-R-210` from `Q_s`; and `difference_sd(i, j, mode) = √(C_ii − 2C_ij + C_jj)` (`BRN52` §8.3.2; `C` of the mode). The cofactor is whatever the solve returned — **with a prior it is the posterior one and `ν` is `EST-R-403`'s**. No parameter is reordered, dropped or renamed on the way.
- **EST-R-505.** **Formal and scaled are never conflated.** Every function that has a covariance takes the mode as an explicit argument with **no default**; the mode "known `σ₀`" uses the formal covariance and `χ²₂`, the mode "estimated `σ₀`" the scaled covariance and `2F(2, ν)` — never `σ̂₀²Q` with `χ²₂`, never `Q` with `F`. A scaled request on a solution with `ν ≤ 0`, or built without a residual square sum, is `EST-F-503`; a scaled covariance that is exactly zero (a perfect fit, `σ̂₀² = 0`) gives a degenerate block, `EST-F-504`.
- **EST-R-506.** **Stated limits, not claims.** (i) The region is the **first-order** one: exact for a linear model (`EST-A-503` measures that against the objective's own profile on the NIST linear sets), and for a nonlinear fit no better than the linearisation — a **banana-shaped** region is not seen (`AND10` §2.5.2); **no claim is made about any orbit fit's region here**, whose nonlinearity bound and finite-difference correlations belong to the exit gate (`EST-Q-009`); (ii) the region is the **marginal** one, from the block `Q_ij` of the full cofactor (the other parameters free, as the data leave them), not the **conditional** one with the other parameters held (whose covariance is `(N_ij)⁻¹`, smaller where the parameters correlate); (iii) the critical values assume Gaussian errors, the weights as stated; (iv) `p` is the coverage probability of the *region* (`c² = 1` is the 39.35 % contour).
- **EST-R-507.** **The tail forms.** Because `1 − p` is not representable for `p` within a few `u` of 1, `chi2_2_critical_from_tail(α)` `= −2 ln α` and `two_f_2_nu_critical_from_tail(α, ν)` `= ν(α^(−2/ν) − 1)` (`α = 1 − p ∈ (0, 1)`, as `ν·expm1(−(2/ν) ln α)`) take the **upper tail** directly; the `p` forms are those with `α = 1 − p` (exact where `1 − p` is, by Sterbenz, for `p ≥ ½`). Every function is self-tested at the edges of its range and far outside the nominal one (`EST-A-501`).
- **EST-R-900.** *(registered 2026-10-06, before any fit exists.)* The exit gate's fit residual is the statistic **S** of §8.1, computed from the fit's own output by the formula there; a fit **reaches** its frozen residual exactly when `S ≤ F` for its row of §8.1, to the digits `oracle/cases.tsv` prints. The test **prints** `S`, `N`, the per-component and radial/along/cross values and the largest epoch residual beside the frozen value, whichever way the comparison falls.

**The L7 exit gate — the fits and the second clause** *(v0.7, 2026-11-27, **registered before any exit-gate code exists**; `EST-R-900` above is kept as written and these add to it; §3.9 states the configuration)*

- **EST-R-901.** **The seventh parameter reaches the registry through a decorator, and no L4 force changes.** `Srp` consumes no registered parameter (`PHPR-R-005`, ruled) and stays so. A **`ScaledForce`** (proposed home: `modules/forcemodel/include/odl/forcemodel/scaled_force.hpp`, which depends on `dyn` alone for this class — `EST-Q-010`) wraps **any** `dyn::Force` and one `ParameterId` `c` issued by L3's registry (kind `srp_scale`, unit `1`, name `srp_scale`, subject the satellite): `accel = c · a_inner`; `d(a)/d(r)` and `d(a)/d(v)` of the inner force **each times `c`** (and the declared neglected-velocity bound times `|c|`); the column of the wrapped force's own parameters times `c`; **`d(a)/d(c) = a_inner` (the acceleration at `c = 1`)**; `consumes() = {c} ∪ the inner force's`. `c` is the multiplier of the template's nominal `A·C_R/m` (the `G-*` convention, `oracle/ORACLE.md` §6): the fitted `c` is reported **and** `c·A·C_R/m`. A non-finite `c`, or a wrapped force already consuming `c`, is `EST-F-902`. With `c = 1` the decorated force is the inner one **bit for bit**.
- **EST-R-902.** **The configuration of §3.9 is stated in the test, once, and printed with every fit**: the gravity degree and order, set A / set B, the tide options and degrees, `extrapolate_secular` and the size of the secular-pole shift it moves, the three relativity terms, the cannonball's area, mass and optics (or the box-wing's block and SVN), the integration tolerance, the start, the estimation epoch, the arc. The variants (degree 12; set B) are **separate fits whose results are printed beside the gated ones and gate nothing**.
- **EST-R-903.** **The data are read whole, or the test refuses.** All 96 epochs of the satellite are read from the pinned SP3 file through `odl::io`'s one SP3 reader; an epoch the file marks absent or bad, a satellite record missing at an epoch, an epoch count other than 96, or epochs that are not 900 s apart refuses the test (`EST-F-901`) **rather than being dropped**. The residual is formed in the ITRS in metres, unit weight (§3.9); `S_N`, `S_{N−7}`, `S/√3`, the radial / along-track / cross-track RMS, the largest epoch residual and the parameters are printed as §8.1 states.
- **EST-R-904.** **What each fit reports about its own iteration.** The status of `LmResult` and its message, the passes (accepted and rejected), `F` and the gradient cosine at the end, `S_N` at the start and at the end (their ratio is the gross-error control of §3.9), the shadow function over the arc (its minimum `ν` and the number of epochs below 1, **printed before the fit**: GPS satellites cross the shadow in their eclipse seasons, and the repaired `dyn::propagate` of `SHDW-F-002` is not what that carried item was about — the carried item is the eclipse-edge Jacobian of `Srp`, `ARC-L-002b` stage d), and the first-step cap's value for the arc (`min(span/100, 0.21 T)`, `INTG-Q-001a`: for the longer half-arc (43 200 s) at a GPS semi-major axis of about 26 560 km, `span/100 ≈ 4.3 × 10² s` against `0.21 T ≈ 9 × 10³ s` (`T ≈ 4.3 × 10⁴ s`, computed here from `T = 2π√(a³/μ)`, not read from a source) — **the cap is not what binds**, stated so that nobody looks for it). **An arc that crosses the shadow is flagged**; for it `EST-A-901` is **reported and not gated** (its Jacobian passes through the shadow's edge, the carried finding), and the Committee rules before the number is read as the exit gate's.
- **EST-R-905.** **The finite-difference correlations (the second clause of the exit gate; `EST-Q-009`).** The sizing is **frozen before any fit** in the form of step 1 (`MEAS62`; `SPEC-forcemodel` §6.2): central differences, `ε(h) = h²F/6 + ν/h`, with `ν` the noise of the evaluated whitened residual **measured from model calls only** (the residual at the starting state repeated with the state moved by amounts far below `h`; the largest spread) and `F` a bound on the third derivative of the residual with respect to each parameter **estimated from a scan of model calls** at the start; the step of parameter `j` is `h_j = (3ν/F_j)^(1/3)` in the parameter's own units, `ε_j = ε(h_j)`. A **sizing tool** computes and prints `ν`, `F_j`, `h_j`, `ε_j` and writes them, with their SHA-256, to a file that is **committed before the fit runs** and read by the test; **the fit may not change a frozen figure**. The independent matrix is `N_FD = J_FDᵀ J_FD` with `J_FD` the central differences of the whitened residual vector (**seven columns, each from the model alone, not from `propagate_with_sensitivities`**), and `ρ_FD = N_FD⁻¹`'s normalised off-diagonal entries, **compared with `JointCovariance::correlation` of the fit's own solve over all `7·6/2 = 21` pairs**.
- **EST-R-906.** **The orbit fit's two-parameter regions against the objective's own profile, with a bound registered first.** The pairs are fixed by **rules applied to the start's covariance, before the fit**: **P1** = `(srp_scale, the state component with the largest |ρ| against it)`; **P2** = `(the two state components with the largest |ρ|)`; the chosen pairs are printed before the profile is run. The level is `p = 0.95`, the mode **`estimated_sigma0`** (`2F(2, ν)`, `EST-R-501`). **The nonlinearity bound is derived, not chosen:** with `Ω(θ̂ + δ) − Ω̂ = δᵀNδ + δᵀH_rδ + O(‖δ‖³)`, `H_r = Σₖ rₖ∇²rₖ` the Hessian term the Gauss–Newton `N` neglects, evaluated at the fit's solution by second central differences of the residual with their own frozen sizing (as `EST-R-905`), the **registered bound** on the relative departure of the profile from the ellipse at radius `s` (`s = 1` the contour) is **`b_pair = 2·|λ|_max(N_s^(−1/2) H_s N_s^(−1/2))·s`**, `N_s`, `H_s` in the scaled variables of `EST-R-201`, `λ` over the pair's block. The profile is `P(θ_ij) = min over the other five parameters of Ω − Ω̂`, computed **with the pair held through the elimination of `EST-R-209`** (the hatch's own purpose) by the same `levenberg_marquardt` from the fit's solution, at `s ∈ {0.5, 1}` on eight points each of the ellipse.
- **EST-R-907.** **Stated limits, not claims.** (i) The frozen `G-*` numbers rank last (§8.1) and detect gross error only; (ii) **a fit that does not reach its frozen number is a finding**, brought to the manager unchanged — not tuned away, and not a failure of the exit gate's *second* clause, which is independent of it; (iii) the region of §3.9 is first-order and `EST-A-902` measures how far the true objective departs from it **for these three arcs only**; (iv) nothing registered here is a statement about any other satellite, arc or force set; (v) the cannonball's `c` and a box-wing's `c` are **different quantities** (the first absorbs the template's `A·C_R/m`, the second is a scale on a computed force) and are never compared as numbers.

## 5. Interfaces · 6. Precision and accuracy

### 5.1 Step 2's interface (module `estimation`, namespace `odl::estimation`, header `odl/estimation/normal_equations.hpp`; the dense kernels in `odl/estimation/dense.hpp`)

```cpp
[[nodiscard]] int scale_exponent(double diagonal) noexcept;            // EST-R-201: floor(k/2), diagonal = m 2^k, 0.5 <= m < 1

struct ConditionReport {                                                // EST-R-207
    double condition_estimate = 0.0;                                    // kappa_est <= kappa_2(N_s)
    double condition_upper    = 0.0;                                    // kappa_up  >= kappa_2(N_s)
    std::vector<double> parameter_conditions;                           // kappa_i = (N_s)_ii (Q_s)_ii, one per parameter; NaN if eliminated
    std::vector<double> weakest_direction;                              // unit vector, scaled variables, one per parameter
};

struct EstimationRefusal : odl::Diagnostic {                            // §7: the id is a literal EST-F-2nn
    std::string parameter;                                              // the parameter named; "" when the message names several
    ConditionReport condition;                                          // whatever could be computed
};

struct Elimination { std::string name; double value; };                 // EST-R-209
struct SolveOptions { std::vector<Elimination> eliminate; };

struct FormedSystem { std::size_t n; std::vector<double> matrix, rhs; };   // N-hat (full, symmetric, row-major) and b-hat: EST-R-203

struct Solution {                                                       // all vectors indexed by parameter unless stated
    std::vector<std::string> names;
    std::vector<double> x;                                              // the correction, unscaled; eliminated parameters at their values
    std::vector<unsigned char> eliminated;
    std::vector<int> scale_exponents;                                   // e_i (0 for eliminated)
    std::vector<std::size_t> free_index;                                // the free parameters' indices, ascending
    std::vector<double> scaled_matrix, scaled_rhs, scaled_solution, scaled_cofactor;   // N_s, b_s, y, Q_s over the FREE parameters
    std::vector<double> cofactor;                                       // Q, n x n, row-major, exactly symmetric
    ConditionReport condition;
    double certified_relative_error = 0.0;                              // B, EST-R-206
    std::size_t observations = 0, free_parameters = 0;
    std::ptrdiff_t degrees_of_freedom = 0;                              // nu = m - n_free
    std::optional<double> weighted_rss;                                 // Omega-hat
    std::string provenance;                                             // EST-R-212
    [[nodiscard]] Result<double, EstimationRefusal> sigma0_squared() const;                 // EST-F-205
    [[nodiscard]] std::vector<double> formal_covariance() const;                            // Q
    [[nodiscard]] Result<std::vector<double>, EstimationRefusal> scaled_covariance() const; // sigma0^2 Q; EST-F-205
    [[nodiscard]] double correlation(std::size_t i, std::size_t j) const;                   // from Q_s; rho_ii = 1
};

class NormalEquations {
public:
    [[nodiscard]] static Result<NormalEquations, EstimationRefusal> create(std::vector<std::string> names);
    [[nodiscard]] static Result<NormalEquations, EstimationRefusal>
    from_normal_system(std::vector<std::string> names, std::span<const double> matrix, std::span<const double> rhs,
                       std::size_t observations, std::optional<double> residual_square_sum = std::nullopt);   // EST-R-213
    [[nodiscard]] Result<void, EstimationRefusal> add(std::span<const double> row, double residual, double sigma);   // EST-R-202
    [[nodiscard]] std::size_t parameters() const noexcept;
    [[nodiscard]] std::size_t observations() const noexcept;
    [[nodiscard]] Result<FormedSystem, EstimationRefusal> formed_system() const;                        // EST-R-203, for the gate's layer (a)
    [[nodiscard]] Result<Solution, EstimationRefusal> solve(const SolveOptions& = {}) const;            // EST-R-201 .. 212
};
```

Nothing in the interface takes or returns an unscaled factorisation, and **no option turns the scaling off** (`EST-R-201`). The kernels of `dense.hpp` (`cholesky`, the two substitutions, products) are public so that the closed-form tests of `EST-A-203` can address them; they are scale-agnostic by nature and **are not reachable from a `NormalEquations`** except through the scaled matrix.

### 5.2 Step 3's interface (module `estimation`, namespace `odl::estimation`, header `odl/estimation/levenberg_marquardt.hpp`; `core`-only, as step 2)

```cpp
struct LmEvaluation {                    // everything WHITENED: F = ½ Σ r²
    std::vector<double> residual;        // r_k = (observed_k − computed_k)/σ_k, m values
    std::vector<double> jacobian;        // A_ki = ∂(computed_k)/∂x_i / σ_k, m×n row-major, THE PARTIALS OF THE MODEL; empty when not asked
    std::optional<double> objective;     // F(x) when it is not ½Σr² (a prior adds to it, step 4)
};
using LmModel = std::function<odl::Result<LmEvaluation, EstimationRefusal>(std::span<const double> x, bool with_jacobian)>;
enum class LmStatus { converged_gradient, converged_residual, converged_step, stalled, max_iterations, refused };
struct LmOptions { double tau = 1.0e-3, gradient_cosine = 1.0e-10, residual_fraction = 1.0e-24, step_tolerance = 1.0e-12, stalled_cosine = 1.0e-4;
                   std::size_t max_iterations = 1000; double mu_max = 1.0e16; };
LmResult levenberg_marquardt(const std::vector<std::string>& names, std::span<const double> x0, const LmModel& model, const LmOptions& options = {});
```

`with_jacobian` is false for a trial point whose Jacobian is not yet needed; it is asked again only if the step is accepted. A refusal returned for a trial is a rejected step (`EST-R-303`), for the start `EST-F-301`. `LmResult` is `EST-R-305`'s record. The parameters are named because step 2 needs names.

### 5.3 Step 4's interface (module `estimation`, namespace `odl::estimation`, new header `odl/estimation/prior.hpp`; `core`-only, as steps 2 and 3 — the module does **not** depend on `frames`) *(v0.5, registered before any code)*

```cpp
struct PriorBlock {                                                     // EST-R-401; built only by the two factories, which validate (EST-F-401 .. EST-F-405)
    std::vector<std::string> names;                                     // isotropic: any number; RTN: exactly three (inertial x, y, z of one vector)
    std::vector<double> centre;                                         // x_A, one per name, the parameters' own units
    std::vector<double> sigma;                                          // isotropic: one per name; RTN: (sigma_R, sigma_T, sigma_N)
    std::optional<std::array<double, 9>> basis;                         // RTN: row-major, rows e_R, e_T, e_N (FRAME-R-040); the matrix frames::rtn_basis returns, element for element
    [[nodiscard]] static odl::Result<PriorBlock, EstimationRefusal>
    isotropic(std::vector<std::string> names, std::vector<double> centre, std::vector<double> sigma);
    [[nodiscard]] static odl::Result<PriorBlock, EstimationRefusal>
    rtn(std::array<std::string, 3> names, std::array<double, 3> centre, const std::array<double, 9>& basis, std::array<double, 3> sigma_rtn);
    [[nodiscard]] std::size_t rows() const noexcept;                    // isotropic: names.size(); RTN: 3
};

// NormalEquations (normal_equations.hpp) gains:
[[nodiscard]] Result<void, EstimationRefusal> add_prior(const PriorBlock&, std::span<const double> linearisation_point);   // EST-R-402: d = fl(x_A - x_lin); EST-F-405, EST-F-406 at solve()
// Solution (EST-R-403) gains:
std::size_t prior_rows = 0;                                             // r
std::optional<double> weighted_rss_data, weighted_rss_prior;            // Omega-hat_data, Omega-hat_prior ; weighted_rss (step 2's field) = their sum when any block was added, unchanged otherwise
// degrees_of_freedom = m + r - n_free (EST-Q-007)

// the damped method (EST-R-404): the prior as rows; levenberg_marquardt.cpp is NOT changed
[[nodiscard]] odl::Result<LmModel, EstimationRefusal> with_priors(LmModel model, std::vector<std::string> names, std::vector<PriorBlock> blocks);
```

`with_priors` itself refuses (`EST-F-401`, `-405`) at construction (`-406` concerns the batch solve's elimination only); **`EST-F-404` is returned by the wrapped model's evaluation** the first time the inner model's answer carries `LmEvaluation::objective` (the model is a callable, so that is the first moment it can be seen): at the start point that is `levenberg_marquardt`'s `refused` with `EST-F-301`'s message, the refusal's own id `EST-F-404` carried inside it. The **caller** converts `rtn_basis(r_A, v_A)` — a `frames::Mat3` — to the nine doubles (a test in `tests/` that links `frames` and `estimation` does it, and **compares the nine element for element** with `frames::rtn_basis`'s output, `EST-A-403`); `estimation` itself never sees a state vector. *(`EST-Q-008`: the manager may instead have `estimation` depend on `frames` and own `rtn_basis(r, v)` here; the registered choice is the `core`-only one, because step 4's evidence needs no state and because a second `rtn_basis` would be a second copy of `FRAME-R-040`.)*

### 5.4 Step 5's interface (module `estimation`, namespace `odl::estimation`, new header `odl/estimation/joint_covariance.hpp`; `core`-only, as steps 2 – 4 — the module does **not** depend on `dyn`) *(v0.6, registered before any code)*

```cpp
enum class ConfidenceMode { known_sigma0, estimated_sigma0 };           // EST-R-505: always passed, never defaulted; known -> formal Q and chi^2_2, estimated -> sigma0^2 Q and 2F(2, nu)

[[nodiscard]] odl::Result<double, EstimationRefusal> chi2_2_critical(double p);                              // EST-R-501: -2 log1p(-p),  p in (0, 1)           EST-F-501
[[nodiscard]] odl::Result<double, EstimationRefusal> two_f_2_nu_critical(double p, std::ptrdiff_t nu);       // EST-R-501: nu expm1(-(2/nu) log1p(-p)),  nu >= 1   EST-F-501   (this is 2F, not F)
[[nodiscard]] odl::Result<double, EstimationRefusal> confidence_critical_value(ConfidenceMode, double p, std::ptrdiff_t nu);   // EST-R-501 (nu is read in the estimated mode only)
[[nodiscard]] odl::Result<double, EstimationRefusal> chi2_2_critical_from_tail(double alpha);                // EST-R-507: -2 ln(alpha),  alpha in (0, 1)
[[nodiscard]] odl::Result<double, EstimationRefusal> two_f_2_nu_critical_from_tail(double alpha, std::ptrdiff_t nu);   // EST-R-507

struct ConfidenceEllipse2D {                                            // EST-R-502
    std::string name_i, name_j;
    ConfidenceMode mode = ConfidenceMode::known_sigma0;
    double p = 0.0, level = 0.0;                                        // p and c^2
    std::array<double, 2> centre{};                                     // theta-hat_ij
    std::array<double, 3> covariance{};                                 // C_ii, C_ij, C_jj of the MODE
    double semi_major = 0.0, semi_minor = 0.0, angle = 0.0;             // a >= b ; angle of the major axis from the i axis, radians, (-pi/2, pi/2]
    [[nodiscard]] double quadratic(std::array<double, 2> displacement) const noexcept;       // v^T C^-1 v
    [[nodiscard]] bool contains(std::array<double, 2> point) const noexcept;                 // quadratic(point - centre) <= level
    [[nodiscard]] std::array<double, 2> boundary_point(double t) const noexcept;             // centre + R(angle) (a cos t, b sin t)
};
[[nodiscard]] odl::Result<ConfidenceEllipse2D, EstimationRefusal>
two_parameter_ellipse(const Solution&, const std::string& name_i, const std::string& name_j, ConfidenceMode, double p, double known_sigma0 = 1.0);   // EST-F-501 .. 504

struct JointParameterLayout {                                           // EST-R-503
    std::array<std::string, 6> state_names;                             // position then velocity
    std::vector<std::string> force_names;                               // L3's registration (issue) order
    [[nodiscard]] static odl::Result<JointParameterLayout, EstimationRefusal> create(std::array<std::string, 6> state, std::vector<std::string> forces);   // EST-F-505
    [[nodiscard]] std::vector<std::string> all() const;                 // state, then forces
};

struct JointCovariance {                                                // EST-R-504
    std::vector<std::string> names;                                     // layout order
    std::vector<double> formal;                                         // Q, n x n row-major
    std::optional<std::vector<double>> scaled;                          // sigma0^2 Q; absent when nu <= 0 or no Omega-hat
    std::vector<double> correlation;                                    // from Q_s, unit-free, n x n
    [[nodiscard]] odl::Result<double, EstimationRefusal> difference_sd(const std::string& i, const std::string& j, ConfidenceMode) const;   // sqrt(C_ii - 2 C_ij + C_jj); EST-F-502, -503
};
[[nodiscard]] odl::Result<JointCovariance, EstimationRefusal> joint_covariance(const Solution&, const JointParameterLayout&);   // EST-F-505
```

The module takes **names**, never a `dyn::ParameterId`: the integration test builds the layout from `ParameterRegistry::declaration(id)->name` for the ids in the order `declare` returned them (`EST-A-506`). Nothing here factors a matrix: the 2 × 2 inverse is the adjugate over the determinant, and the eigen-structure of a 2 × 2 is closed-form. *(v0.6a, 2026-11-27, TREE-10 — recorded, no number changes: `JointCovariance` also carries `std::vector<unsigned char> eliminated`, one flag per parameter in layout order, 1 for an eliminated parameter (its row and column of `Q` are exactly zero); `difference_sd` needs it for `EST-F-502`. The v0.6 listing above omitted it; the implementation has had it since its first run.)*

### 6.1 Budget rows (step 2)

| id | quantity | budget | because |
|---|---|---|---|
| `EST-P-3` | the factorisation and the two substitutions, in the scaled 2-norm, **leading term** of `Θ_s(n)` at `n = 6` (`3n² + n = 114`) | 114 × 1.1102 × 10⁻¹⁶ = **1.2656 × 10⁻¹⁴** | `DEM89`'s proof, `u = 2⁻⁵³`; the full `Θ_s` multiplies by `χₙ` and adds `n³u²` (§3.4) |
| `EST-P-4` | the forming of `N` at `m = 40` rows (Pontius), leading term of `γₘ` | 40 × 1.1102 × 10⁻¹⁶ = **4.4408 × 10⁻¹⁵** | the dot-product bound; relative to `√(NᵢᵢNⱼⱼ)` by Cauchy–Schwarz |
| `EST-P-5` | the representation error of a decimal datum rounded to double (through x87 long double, once), doubled for a product, **`η_rep`** | 2.01 × 1.1102 × 10⁻¹⁶ = **2.2315 × 10⁻¹⁶** | `u` plus 4 × 10⁻¹⁹ for the extended-precision recurrence, twice |
| `EST-P-6` | the certified values' own last-digit uncertainty, worst case (leading digit 1) | **1 × 10⁻¹⁴** relative | one unit of NIST's 15th printed digit; the exact solutions of the normal equations of the eleven datasets agree with the certified values to 14.3 – 15.4 digits (`tools/estimation_sizing.cpp`) |
| `EST-P-7` | the factor of `B` over `q₁ Θ_N` | 2 × 2 = **4** | `‖A⁻¹‖₂ ≤ 2‖N_s⁻¹‖₂` (scaled diagonal below 2) and `‖N_s⁻¹‖₂ ≤ 2q₁` (`EST-R-206`) |
| `EST-P-8` | **the smallest relative defect each layer can see** (a defect in the layer's own result that exceeds its bound): the solve (layer b) at `n = 3`, leading term | 30 × 1.1102 × 10⁻¹⁶ = **3.3306 × 10⁻¹⁵** | `Θ_s(3)`; the forming (layer a) at `m = 40` is `EST-P-4`; the certified-value layer's resolution is its `B` (§6.2: 1.5 × 10⁻¹¹ at Pontius, 3.7 × 10⁻⁵ at Longley) — **the forward gate is the loosest of the four, by `κ`, and the κ-free layers (a), (b) are what see a defect near the roundoff level** |
| `EST-P-9` | **prediction, not a criterion** (rule 7, the middle form): the scaled backward error of layer (b) at `n = 1 … 7` on the ten served datasets, with an exact residual | ≤ 4 × 1.1102 × 10⁻¹⁶ = **4.4408 × 10⁻¹⁶** (measured in the exploratory runs of PROVENANCE §40.7: 0.06 – 1.6 `u`, independent of `κ`) | `DEM89`'s backward stability with a constant of order 1; the registered bound `Θ_s` is `n + 3n²` times larger |
| `EST-P-10` | **prediction, not a criterion:** the observed scaled error of layer (c) against its registered `B`, ten datasets | ≤ **5 × 10⁻²** of `B` (exploratory: ≤ 4 × 10⁻² at the two NoInt datasets, whose `B` is dominated by the certified values' own 10⁻¹⁴, and ≤ 2.5 × 10⁻³ elsewhere) | `B` is a worst-case bound whose looseness grows with `κ`; the exploratory runs show it |

### 6.2 The registered figures (generated; `tools/estimation_sizing.cpp`, ctest `estimation.spec_table_matches_generator`)

**Everything here is frozen before any code of the estimator exists.** The tool reproduces it from the pinned cache; `modules/estimation/tests/registered_figures.hpp` carries the same figures as hexadecimal floats and is checked against the generator by the ctest `estimation.sizing_header_matches_generator`. A figure that moves — because a datum, a formula or the recipe changed — fails those two tests: **a registration is not edited after a run**.

<!-- BEGIN estimation_sizing: generated by tools/estimation_sizing.cpp --spec-table; do not edit by hand -->

**The registered figures, per dataset** (NIST StRD linear regression, read from the pinned cache; `m` rows, `n` parameters; the design recipe of §8.2; `u = 2⁻⁵³`). `e` are the scale exponents of `EST-R-201`; `κ` is the exact `κ₂(N_s)` of the power-of-two-scaled matrix; `κᵢ = Nᵢᵢ (N⁻¹)ᵢᵢ`; `q₁ = ‖Q_s‖₁`; `Θ_N` the forming-and-solving constant of `EST-R-206` at η = 0; `B`(code) the relative error the solve certifies; `B`(test) the same with the representation error of decimal data and the certified values' own last digit added; `B_Q` the bound on `|Q̂ᵢᵢ/Qᵢᵢ − 1|`; `B_σ` the bound on `|σ̂₀ − σ₀|`; the last column what the factorisation of the scaled matrix does.

| dataset | `n × m` | `e` | `κ` | `min κᵢ` – `max κᵢ` | `q₁` | `Θ_N` | `B` (code) | `B` (test) | `B_Q` | `B_σ` | outcome |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Norris | 2 × 36 | 3, 12 | 7.88 | 2.49 – 2.49 | 7.67 | 9.55 × 10⁻¹⁵ | 4.66 × 10⁻¹³ | 5.00 × 10⁻¹³ | 4.42 × 10⁻¹⁴ | 6.22 × 10⁻¹³ | completes |
| Pontius | 3 × 40 | 3, 23, 45 | 3.53 × 10² | 1.11 × 10¹ – 7.64 × 10¹ | 1.49 × 10² | 1.67 × 10⁻¹⁴ | 1.45 × 10⁻¹¹ | 1.51 × 10⁻¹¹ | 2.15 × 10⁻¹² | 1.82 × 10⁻¹⁵ | completes |
